Anja's lyric sheets had disappeared. Quell called the delay a rotten example of artistic vanity and threatened another pink slip. Cassian asked him to put the threat in writing. The organizer suddenly preferred to discuss something else.","Mara found the missing pages beneath the portraiture banner. Anja's masculine costume from last year had been cut out and pinned to its corner, as if the singer were merchandise with interchangeable features. The joke was insensitive rather than clever. Anja folded the cutout and put it aside. \"I still like that suit,\" she said. \"I won't let him take that too.\"","Quell's new memorandum announced a nominal cancellation. His memo described it as an effort to preempt disorder under the hall's founding resolution. June read the old charter aloud, including the promise of social intercourse. A rule supporting artistic conversation was a poor basis for forbidding it. The reinterpretation was propaganda dressed in formal prose.","The poet arrived with his journal and a proposed poem. Its preface contained a fiery oath, a plea for solidarity, and a proverb about taking the wall down. Mara asked whether he could precede the poem with a simple account of what had happened. He grimaced. Brevity was not his preferred art, but he was keen to help Anja rather than become the main event.","He tried a fresh metaphor. \"The hall is a stolen passport.\"","\"The passport isn't stolen,\" she said. \"The name check is wrong.\"","The poet's temper was equally changeable. He asked June to weigh in on a pronoun in his verse, then complained that careful poetry took forever. 'An uncool discovery,' she said. 'Literature has edits.' The assistant's snort was of note: it was the first time Mara had heard her laugh. Oceanic Arts could keep its grand logo; the people beneath it were becoming skeptical.","He scratched out the line. His willingness to revise mattered more than his first inspiration. Quell had confused being questioned with being ill-treated; the poet did not have to repeat that mistake. He wrote a satire in which the doorkeeper forgot who had built the door. It was a better riddle, and a more accurate one.","A technician offered a quiet query. The portal's patent license covered a recording tool, not the employment decisions Quell had attributed to it. Its owner had requested review of a particular recording, not Anja's dismissal. June asked for the original message. The technician enabled her to read the full chain and agreed to preserve a copy for the hall's board.","\"I'm not giving legal advice,\" he said. \"I'm saying the software didn't send the pink slip.\"","A useful distinction. The pronouncement about legislation had come from Quell's own office. His periodical interview claimed the hall enjoyed round-the-clock technical review, but the duty log showed no check of this entry before dismissal. His party-line assurance had relied on nobody comparing his statements.","Mara examined the nickname field. A plural marker had been added after Anja's name. A separate translator treated it as a noun, although it was a person's name. The intimation in the error report was that she had entered it incorrectly. The log showed Quell's assistant had imported it. One mistaken preposition in the assistant's note had then changed \"booked by Anja\" into \"booked for Anja,\" making her appear to be a customer rather than a performer.","The assistant stood nearby, obedient and miserable. She had followed instructions to alter the record after Anja refused to remove the song. An intensive rehearsal schedule and fear of dismissal had made obedience seem easier than a question. She offered the original export. The subsequent misinterpretation was deliberate, even if the first typing error was not.","Quell entered, ordered her out, and demanded a sanction against anyone revealing private records. June would publish no passport number. The assistant's own account could be given to the board without displaying unrelated personal details. Quell's concern had not appeared when he circulated Anja's document to sponsors. That irony was not lost on the crew.","A child from the offline exhibit wandered in with the Latin quiz card. The pronunciation of his answer made Anja laugh for the first time all evening. \"That sounds like my grandmother,\" she said. \"She gave every dead language a local accent.\"","Mara was wary when Quell raised his voice. He expected the assistant to react forcefully, giving him an excuse to remove her. She did not. The paradox of being brave while standing still seemed to surprise him. His speech could be compared to a machine producing plentiful noise and no answer. The corrected message was ready for propagation, with a second saved copy for redundancy.","He invited her to try the pop-up game on Internet Explorer. She promised to come later. A place that could welcome old computers ought to survive an unfamiliar name. Cassian's retort was a soft one, aimed only at Mara: \"She makes a better argument than the entire board.\"","The board chair finally arrived, having received Anja's petition through the postal service as well as e-mail. She had never seen the version opposing all visiting artists. The misinformation had come from a mailing sent under the hall's account. She read the source, checked the timing, and asked Quell to step away from the event while the staff reviewed the conduct.","Quell tried persuasion, then a boast about the audience turnout, then a threat to turn from generous supporter to ruthless competitor. His rhetoric drew no immediate reward. He would not reap another night's applause from the people he had silenced. The chair authorized Anja's booking for the evening while the wider contract dispute was reviewed.","The band resumed. In the wings, the poet rehearsed a short recitation. His lyric had become a novel little hybrid, half song and half spoken challenge. The jargon of programming had vanished from the stage; the actual mistake remained clear. A wrong name had become a pretext for punishing a performer.","June asked Anja whether she wanted an onstage refutation of the rumors. Anja preferred a brief introduction, then her own work. The poem would not stand in for her voice. Neither would the well-meaning anger of people who wanted the status quo overturned in a single night.","Mara settled beside Cassian, who took her hand. His salute to her victory was a kiss across her knuckles. Quell's scenario had seemed massive an hour earlier. Now it looked shabby, built from small lies and other people's compliance. Subsequently, the room filled with music."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1097,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0170\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：The Night of Names</div><h1>The Song They Wanted Removed</h1><p id=\"chapter-0170-p1\">Backstage, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4583\" data-words=\"4583\">jazz</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-743\" data-words=\"743\">band</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">was</a> arguing <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9260\" data-words=\"9260\">with</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6727\" data-words=\"6727\">rap</a> trio <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5846\" data-words=\"5846\">over</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5505\" data-words=\"5505\">next</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5393\" data-words=\"5393\">musical</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2040\" data-words=\"2040\">cue</a>. The jazz arrangement left space around the first lyric, and the room grew quiet enough to hear her breathe. Cassian leaned toward Mara. \"An excellent argument for the musical evening we nearly missed.\"","She put his hand against her cheek. \"You're allowed to enjoy a night without rescuing anyone.\"","\"I'll try not to make it a habit.\"","Her laugh was lost beneath the first chord. Onstage, the singer delivered the song Quell had wanted removed. Its metaphor of a toll on a borrowed voice was clear without naming a company. A patent dispute would not be settled by applause. Her performance simply refused to let a private threat decide what she could say that evening.","The poet's recitation followed. He read a brief preface stating that his work concerned a local decision, not every rule in the hall's history. The prose account helped listeners understand the satire. His new poem gave its doorkeeper a passport full of stamps and no place to sit. Anja had allowed him that invented image after the riddle ceased pretending to describe her literal document.","A proverb from his grandmother closed the piece: a roof was only useful if someone could enter. It was ordinary wisdom, delivered without an oath to destroy anyone. The audience gave him a salute that left him almost unable to bow. He had wanted to reap praise; he found himself more pleased that Anja laughed at the right line.","Backstage, June checked the corrected memorandum. The memo no longer attributed Quell's decision to legislation. It described a nominal fee, an incorrect entry, and an unauthorized removal. The board's resolution would be reviewed before any lasting sanction was imposed. A careful pronouncement could preempt fresh misinformation without pretending the contract dispute had vanished.","Anja's plea had reached people who had never attended before. They would turn out again, she hoped, for the poetry rather than the scandal. Mara remained skeptical of promises shouted after a concert, but she loved the singer's bright face. No rotten little misinterpretation should have stolen that expression from her. She kissed her on the cheek and made her laugh.","The assistant sat with her hands folded. She had feared another pink slip, but the chair asked her to document what she had changed first. The intimation that she alone had caused the trouble was already circulating. Her journal of daily work, kept to remember instructions, recorded the order to alter Anja's booking. She would provide the relevant pages without handing over her whole private life.","The party-line account from Oceanic Arts acknowledged receipt of the complaint. It was not a refutation, nor did it affirm every claim in the singer's petition. June reported it as the limited statement it was. She would weigh in with a fuller story after the board answered her query about the missing original message. Her periodical had room for a chronology; her first report did not need a triumphant final verdict.","Mara helped Anja retrieve the passport from the office envelope. The hall had no further reason to retain it. Its name had not changed because a nickname field was wrong. The record's plural marker would be corrected, and staff would stop guessing whether an unfamiliar name was a noun to translate. A pronoun in the revised notice now referred clearly to the singer, while an unnecessary preposition had been removed. Small revisions made the account less confusing. Anja disliked the insensitive jokes about her suburban choir and asked him to stop their propagation.","The Latin quiz at the exhibit had attracted a queue. The child with the unusual pronunciation challenged Cassian to a match. Mara watched her husband lose on the third pop-up, then accuse the offline Internet Explorer display of being older and wiser than he was. The boy called him uncool. Cassian accepted the judgment with a solemn nod, which only improved his standing.","In the next room, a rap trio tried a rhythm from Anja's song. She joined them, turning the original lyric into an answer. The performance was neither a recital of jargon nor a lecture about persuasion. It was pleasure taking space back. The poet opened his journal to record the rhythm, then closed it and listened instead.","The restored portraiture banner hung behind the bar. Anja had asked that the old masculine suit remain, with a different caption written by her. Her appearance was not evidence that she approved every decision attached to the image. The paradox of advertising a bold artist while demanding her obedience had become embarrassingly visible. Nobody needed an extra retort to complete it.","The scenario Quell had rehearsed depended on everyone being too polite to react. His rhetoric had failed when a quiet assistant refused to help him ill-treat a colleague. That seemed a better ending than a perfect speech. The poet, keen to join a literature circle that did not fear jokes, invited her to his next meeting. She accepted before he could become solemn.","Cassian returned with two glasses. \"A toast to the status quo surviving badly.\"","\"And to better changes,\" Mara said.","The irony made him smile. They did not need to pretend that one performance had rewritten every contract. A lyric could precede a meeting, inspire a petition, and still leave hard work for Monday. The hall's old promise of social intercourse had acquired an audience willing to protect it.","The chair would subsequently review the postal service receipts alongside the electronic records. The direct delivery date mattered because the dismissal had preceded the supposed error report. The pretext was of note even if the eventual employment process took longer. Quell's propaganda had relied on people treating an official envelope as a complete explanation.","At the last musical number, Mara drew Cassian onto the floor. He resisted just long enough to make her pull harder. Then his hand settled at her back, certain and warm. The room was crowded, the dancing inelegant, and her husband's grin devastating.","\"A novel way to end an intensive investigation,\" he said.","\"You're repeating the poet's title.\"","\"I may owe him royalties.\"","She laughed into his collar. Behind them, the drummer struck the final beat. saw her husband hand a beautiful woman a small velvet box. The woman kissed his cheek. Mara stared until the driver asked whether she wanted the next signpost. \"The theater,\" she said. Her voice was singularly calm, which should have warned anyone who knew her. Speculation was becoming dangerously tempting.","Cassian had promised tickets to the Royal Shakespeare Company. He had also spent the morning answering secret calls in an alarming shorthand. The topic, he insisted, was a tribute. Mara was willing to weigh up an innocent explanation. She was less willing to be deprived of the pleasure of surprising him first.","The theater's advisory board had hired a banqueting room for an anniversary show. A Spanish singer named Lena, raised in a southwestern coastal town, was rehearsing an ancient ballad. Her vowel sounds floated through a loose door. A retired actor corrected one tense in the translation and asked whether the verb had lost its subject. The script was a deliberately ridiculous thriller about an abdication, a missing visa, and a treaty stolen by a dentist. The Supreme Court appeared in the second act, represented by three cardboard owls.","Mara's first impression was not improved by that last detail. A comprehensive catalog called the show an experimental comedy. A cartoon judge on the cover waved a telegram above a caption about temporal confusion. The subheading promised \"Love beyond Reason.\" It would either be an acclaim-worthy triumph or a waste of everybody's evening.","The catalog's singular boast was a promise of international acclaim. Its director, a man who typically hated publicity, called the surprise a spur to better work. In the next room, a child practiced the alphabet while two adults argued about an adverb. Their chatter did more to convey the company's ordinary life than any grand announcement.","An assistant recognized her and let out a yell. \"You're early!\"","\"My usual curse.\"","His name was Theo. He carried an album of set photographs, a dictionary of Spanish idioms, and a dental mirror. The mirror was used to inspect a hidden catch beneath the stage throne. Mara asked whether this was the bargaining tribute Cassian had mentioned. Theo looked so miserable that she nearly forgave him before he answered.","\"He wanted to subsidise the youth company,\" he said. \"A very generous concession. Not a materialistic display at all.\"","\"I hadn't suggested one.\"","He began to dissolve under her gaze. The point person was supposedly Lena, but she had disappeared with the velvet box. Mara followed the trail past a database terminal holding the show's cue sheet. An accidental burst of audio filled the corridor: Cassian's voice saying, \"You mustn't let her guess.\"","With opening imminent, Lena wanted a designation of responsibility. Theo could cite the receipt but could not offer a useful critique of his own decision. He had wasted the morning trying to hide an empty box. Cassian asked everyone to shift to the actual search. A minute of deliberation would suffice; bargaining over blame would not keep the show afloat.","An acute, unreasonable jealousy struck Mara. She knew his love; she also knew the little ugly stories a frightened imagination could produce. Her wit deserted her for exactly three seconds. Then she noticed a benchmark test showing the sound had been recorded yesterday for the performance. The assertion was not a private confession. She would not let a fragment predetermine her account of the man who had trusted her with his home.","Theo offered an anecdote about the costume designer's doctorate. Mara refused the diversion. If her husband was planning a surprise, she could leave. But Theo's hands were shaking for another reason. He admitted that the star jewel from the velvet box had vanished. It was an heirloom Lena intended to pass down, not a theatrical replica. Cassian had arranged its safe presentation after a broken clasp was repaired.","The cue record placed Lena in the wardrobe at noon. A skilled dresser confirmed it. The only other visitor was a foreign-born courier hired to despatch an envelope to the hotel. He had left a coupon for a cinema and an ad for off-plan apartments. Theo flushed at the advertisement. He had reserved one of those flats with his fiancée and feared losing the deposit. It made the missing jewel look particularly bad for him.","Mara looked at the rounded dent in the box's lining. Its obscure little mark was shaped like a bird's claw. A line of verse about a robber seemed unhelpfully prophetic. 'Somebody will characterise this as ridiculous,' she said. Cassian's answering utterance was a laugh. At least the new statute of their marriage permitted her comparative lack of solemnity. She felt encouraged enough to dare a joke.","\"The jewel's loss could be damaging,\" he said. \"And I borrowed the box.\"","Mara understood the worry. His landlord could readily characterize a theater worker as unreliable. His own self-doubt needed no help. But an unwise property choice did not prove an illegal act. She asked for a chronicle of his morning instead of accepting his panic as a confession.","Cassian entered through the stage door carrying flowers. He stopped when he saw her. Then his eyes moved to Theo's empty hands, and his expression changed. \"Oh.\"","\"An insightful summary,\" Mara said.","His laugh escaped before he could stop it. \"I meant to surprise you.\"","\"You've done very well.\"","He looked at the velvet case, now empty. The performance's dedication was written on a card beneath its lining. Mara recognized her name. It was an absurdly extravagant gesture for their anniversary month, and her anger began to feel increasingly misplaced.","Lena returned, vocal and furious. She wanted to call the police. Mara agreed, then asked the staff to keep the doors clear. An under-performing lift had trapped the courier's trolley near the service stairs; the man had left it there. A rear office window stood open above the alley. Lena's cry brought a cheer from an actor who mistook it for his cue.","\"No more signaling,\" Mara said. \"The next yell had better mean something.\"","Cassian offered his arm. \"May I help?\"","She took it. \"You may explain the dentist while we search.\"","\"That,\" he said, \"may exceed even my talents.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1039,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0172\" <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">he</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-7270\" data-words=\"7270\">said</a>, &quot;<a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5113\" data-words=\"5113\">may</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-2971\" data-words=\"2971\">exceed</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-2927\" data-words=\"2927\">even</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-5401\" data-words=\"5401\">my</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8276\" data-words=\"8276\">talents</a>.&quot;</p><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"170\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"172\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x026c02","title":"The Feather in the Lift","volume":"婚后外传：A Tribute with Feathers","anchor":"chapter-0173","paragraphs":["The courier's trolley held costumes, a bag of artificial snow, and a telegram addressed to the Supreme Court. Mara removed the last item before opening the wheel housing. A small golden glint lay inside. Cassian leaned closer. The star's chain had caught around the axle, but the jewel itself was gone. Speculation about temporal order would wait; Mara needed a verb that meant act.","\"Somebody wanted us to find the chain,\" he said.","Cassian had an acute sense of how badly the surprise could go wrong. The singular mercy was Mara's restored wit. Her first utterance about the bird became a spur to practical action: 'Find somewhere a sensible thief would hide something bright.' He promised not to characterise the creature before meeting it.","\"Or somebody wanted it hidden and chose badly. Let's weigh up the wheel marks.\"","The trolley had crossed wet paint near a signpost pointing toward the wardrobe. The courier said he never entered that room. His translation app had directed him to a different building, but the theater's database recorded his actual route. Mara asked him to wait for the officers rather than predetermine the explanation. He agreed so readily that Theo began to breathe again.","Lena's Spanish ballad was still playing through the speakers. The audio loop had become a curse to the people searching beneath the stage. Its southwestern verse described a girl following a silver bird. A singer's vocal power could make a simple lyric feel like prophecy; Mara preferred the useful detail of a trolley wheel. She asked a skilled stagehand to lift it while Cassian held the frame.","The wheel housing contained a torn cinema coupon. It matched the courier's ad, but the paper's ink had bled into fresh blue paint. The chain lay on top of it, dry. Someone had added the jewelry after the trolley stopped. The discovery characterized the hiding place differently. The courier could still have done it, yet the first assertion about his route no longer sufficed.","Cassian found a carton containing the show's treaty, statute, and visa props. The dictionary, open beside it, had a penciled pronunciation note about a difficult vowel. Beneath that lay a shorthand message: STAR TO ROOF. Its subheading read \"Act Three,\" but the script had only two acts. The apparent signaling was an amateur invention.","The director consulted his advisory notes, then offered a concession: the whole absurd chronicle could go in the souvenir album. His doctorate in drama had not prepared him for this topic. He could cite three plays about an abdication, but none about a jackdaw. The tense atmosphere eased when he tried to imitate its voice.","Theo admitted that he had written it. He meant to carry Lena's repaired star to a roof garden for his own proposal after the show. She had offered him the empty presentation box, not the heirloom. His fiancée loved the theater; the off-plan apartment had been intended as another surprise. He had realized too late that surprising somebody with a financial commitment might be less romantic than he thought.","\"She hasn't agreed to buy it?\" Lena asked.","\"I used my savings, not hers.\"","\"Then tell her before you invite her to admire the view.\"","Mara liked that advice. She had no urge to turn a damaging mistake into a moral thriller, but Theo needed to stop treating fear as a reason to improvise. He had moved the box to the trolley when he heard Lena asking for it. He thought an empty case would look like theft. His attempted concealment had made it look exactly that way.","\"The jewel was already missing,\" he said. \"I swear.\"","A yell came from the under-performing lift. The stagehand had freed its gate and discovered a feather caught in the sill. The theater kept no birds, but a jackdaw had been nesting above the loading bay. Lena stared at the open window. She remembered setting the jewel on the costume table while she checked the repaired clasp. Cassian's expensive intervention had not included closing the window.","Mara looked up. A black head appeared along the gutter, then vanished. She wanted to laugh and did not dare; Lena might throw her own passport after the bird. The officers arrived in time to see Cassian climb a maintenance ladder with a stagehand beside him. There was no illegal chase across roofs, only a controlled ascent to a hatch that opened onto the flat service platform.","Mara used the dental mirror to inspect the gap under a drain guard. A star shone among twigs. The jackdaw's accidental collection would not dissolve because she asked politely. She borrowed the prop hook and, with the stagehand's guidance, drew the chainless jewel toward the opening. Her comparative lack of patience nearly cost her the prize. Cassian steadied her elbow, and she slowed.","\"A singularly stubborn thief,\" he murmured.","\"Excellent taste.\"","The bird protested from a safe distance. She handed Lena the star and received an embrace so forceful that her feet nearly left the roof.","Downstairs, the comprehensive search ended in delighted chatter. A cartoon drawn for the noticeboard showed a bird presiding over the Supreme Court. It was a better critique than any speech. The designation of Theo as chief suspect dissolved with the feathers, though his deception about the box remained his to explain. The courier accepted the company's apology and left an address for the official despatch of his receipt.","The show could stay afloat. Its budget benchmark had never allowed for replacing a jewel. Cassian would still subsidise the company, but there would be deliberation about open windows before another heirloom appeared.
